/*
 * #%L
 * ImageJ software for multidimensional image processing and analysis.
 * %%
 * Copyright (C) 2009 - 2025 Board of Regents of the University of
 * Wisconsin-Madison, Broad Institute of MIT and Harvard, and Max Planck
 * Institute of Molecular Cell Biology and Genetics.
 * %%
 * Redistribution and use in source and binary forms, with or without
 * modification, are permitted provided that the following conditions are met:
 *
 * 1. Redistributions of source code must retain the above copyright notice,
 *    this list of conditions and the following disclaimer.
 * 2. Redistributions in binary form must reproduce the above copyright notice,
 *    this list of conditions and the following disclaimer in the documentation
 *    and/or other materials provided with the distribution.
 *
 * THIS SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED BY THE COPYRIGHT HOLDERS AND CONTRIBUTORS "AS IS"
 * AND ANY EXPRESS OR IMPLIED WARRANTIES, INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, THE
 * IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE
 * ARE DISCLAIMED. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE COPYRIGHT HOLDERS OR CONTRIBUTORS BE
 * LIABLE FOR ANY DIRECT, INDIRECT, INCIDENTAL, SPECIAL, EXEMPLARY, OR
 * CONSEQUENTIAL DAMAGES (INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, PROCUREMENT OF
 * SUBSTITUTE GOODS OR SERVICES; LOSS OF USE, DATA, OR PROFITS; OR BUSINESS
 * INTERRUPTION) HOWEVER CAUSED AND ON ANY THEORY OF LIABILITY, WHETHER IN
 * CONTRACT, STRICT LIABILITY, OR TORT (INCLUDING NEGLIGENCE OR OTHERWISE)
 * ARISING IN ANY WAY OUT OF THE USE OF THIS SOFTWARE, EVEN IF ADVISED OF THE
 * POSSIBILITY OF SUCH DAMAGE.
 * #L%
 */

package net.imagej.updater.maven;

import java.util.ArrayDeque;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.Collection;
import java.util.Collections;
import java.util.Comparator;
import java.util.Deque;
import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.HashSet;
import java.util.LinkedHashSet;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.Set;
import java.util.regex.Matcher;
import java.util.regex.Pattern;

/**
 * Minimum Version Selection (MVS) mediation over a composed
 * {@link ComponentCatalog}.
 * <p>
 * This is the cross-site composition algorithm specified in
 * doc/maven-native-update-sites.md §4.1/§4.2, and must produce results
 * identical to the normative reference implementation ({@code dbxm.mvs}
 * in the db-xml-maven repository). Both are validated against the shared
 * golden vectors.
 * </p>
 * <ul>
 * <li>The installation is a synthetic root; user-selected components are
 * its direct dependencies ("roots").</li>
 * <li>Selection per {@link GA} is the highest version requested via any
 * surviving (non-excluded) path; because published edges carry concrete
 * versions only, MVS and highest-wins coincide.</li>
 * <li>Precedence: user pin &gt; BOM ({@code <managed>}) &gt; MVS.</li>
 * <li>Exclusions are per-path; root exclusions are global; BOM-entry
 * exclusions apply to the managed component's outgoing edges. Exclusions
 * never remove an explicitly selected root.</li>
 * <li>The walk traverses each component at its <em>selected</em> version,
 * so losing subtrees are pruned.</li>
 * </ul>
 */
public final class MVSResolver {

    /**
     * Iteration cap for the fixpoint loop. Real dependency graphs converge
     * in a handful of iterations; the cap guards against pathological
     * oscillation.
     */
    public static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = 1000;

    private static final Pattern MAJOR = Pattern.compile("^(\\d+)");

    /** A user-selected component: site current version, or a pinned one. */
    public static final class Root {

        private final GA ga;
        private final String version;
        private final boolean pinned;

        public Root(final GA ga, final String version, final boolean pinned) {
            this.ga = ga;
            this.version = version;
            this.pinned = pinned;
        }

        public GA ga() { return ga; }
        public String version() { return version; }
        public boolean pinned() { return pinned; }
    }

    public enum WarningType {
            HELD_BELOW("held-below"), //
            MAJOR_MISALIGNMENT("major-misalignment"), //
            MISSING_RELEASE("missing-release");

        private final String label;

        WarningType(final String label) {
            this.label = label;
        }

        public String label() { return label; }

        public static WarningType fromLabel(final String label) {
            for (final WarningType type : values()) {
                if (type.label.equals(label)) return type;
            }
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Unknown warning type: " + label);
        }
    }

    /** A non-blocking mediation diagnostic. */
    public static final class MediationWarning {

        private final WarningType type;
        private final GA ga;

        public MediationWarning(final WarningType type, final GA ga) {
            this.type = type;
            this.ga = ga;
        }

        public WarningType type() { return type; }
        public GA ga() { return ga; }

        @Override
        public boolean equals(final Object o) {
            if (!(o instanceof MediationWarning)) return false;
            final MediationWarning other = (MediationWarning) o;
            return type == other.type && ga.equals(other.ga);
        }

        @Override
        public int hashCode() {
            return type.hashCode() * 31 + ga.hashCode();
        }

        @Override
        public String toString() {
            return type.label() + "(" + ga + ")";
        }
    }

    public static final class Resolution {

        private final Map<GA, String> selected;
        private final List<MediationWarning> warnings;
        private final Map<GA, Set<String>> requests;

        Resolution(final Map<GA, String> selected,
            final List<MediationWarning> warnings,
            final Map<GA, Set<String>> requests)
        {
            this.selected = Collections.unmodifiableMap(selected);
            this.warnings = Collections.unmodifiableList(warnings);
            this.requests = Collections.unmodifiableMap(requests);
        }

        /** Final selection: one version per reachable {@link GA}. */
        public Map<GA, String> selected() { return selected; }

        /** Deduplicated, deterministically ordered warnings. */
        public List<MediationWarning> warnings() { return warnings; }

        /** Surviving requests from the stable walk, for diagnostics. */
        public Map<GA, Set<String>> requests() { return requests; }
    }

    public Resolution resolve(final ComponentCatalog catalog,
        final List<Root> roots, final Collection<Exclusion> rootExclusions)
    {
        final Map<GA, String> pins = new HashMap<>();
        for (final Root root : roots) {
            if (root.pinned()) pins.put(root.ga(), root.version());
        }

        Map<GA, String> selected = new HashMap<>();
        Map<GA, Set<String>> requests = null;
        boolean converged = false;
        for (int i = 0; i < MAX_ITERATIONS; i++) {
            requests = walk(catalog, roots, rootExclusions, selected);
            final Map<GA, String> newSelected = new HashMap<>();
            for (final Map.Entry<GA, Set<String>> entry : requests.entrySet()) {
                final GA ga = entry.getKey();
                String version = highest(entry.getValue());
                if (pins.containsKey(ga)) version = pins.get(ga);
                else {
                    final ManagedEntry managed = catalog.managed().get(ga);
                    if (managed != null) version = managed.version();
                }
                newSelected.put(ga, version);
            }
            if (newSelected.equals(selected)) {
                converged = true;
                break;
            }
            selected = newSelected;
        }
        if (!converged) {
            throw new IllegalStateException(
                "MVS did not converge within " + MAX_ITERATIONS + " iterations");
        }

        return new Resolution(selected, warnings(catalog, selected, requests),
            requests);
    }

    // -- Walk --

    private static final class Frame {

        final GA ga;
        final String requested;
        final Set<Exclusion> exclusions;

        Frame(final GA ga, final String requested,
            final Set<Exclusion> exclusions)
        {
            this.ga = ga;
            this.requested = requested;
            this.exclusions = exclusions;
        }
    }

    private static final class State {

        final GA ga;
        final String version;
        final Set<Exclusion> exclusions;

        State(final GA ga, final String version,
            final Set<Exclusion> exclusions)
        {
            this.ga = ga;
            this.version = version;
            this.exclusions = exclusions;
        }

        @Override
        public boolean equals(final Object o) {
            if (!(o instanceof State)) return false;
            final State other = (State) o;
            return ga.equals(other.ga) && version.equals(other.version) &&
                exclusions.equals(other.exclusions);
        }

        @Override
        public int hashCode() {
            return (ga.hashCode() * 31 + version.hashCode()) * 31 +
                exclusions.hashCode();
        }
    }

    /**
     * One traversal from the synthetic root, honoring the current
     * selection. Returns the requested versions per {@link GA} discovered
     * via surviving paths.
     */
    private Map<GA, Set<String>> walk(final ComponentCatalog catalog,
        final List<Root> roots, final Collection<Exclusion> rootExclusions,
        final Map<GA, String> selected)
    {
        final Map<GA, Set<String>> requests = new HashMap<>();
        final Deque<Frame> stack = new ArrayDeque<>();
        final Set<Exclusion> globalExclusions = rootExclusions == null ? //
            Collections.<Exclusion> emptySet() : //
            Collections.unmodifiableSet(new HashSet<>(rootExclusions));

        // Roots are seeded unconditionally: exclusions never remove an
        // explicitly selected root.
        for (final Root root : roots) {
            request(requests, root.ga(), root.version());
            stack.push(new Frame(root.ga(), root.version(), globalExclusions));
        }

        // Visited keys include the exclusion set: the same node may be
        // pruned differently on different paths, and only surviving paths
        // request.
        final Set<State> visited = new HashSet<>();

        while (!stack.isEmpty()) {
            final Frame frame = stack.pop();
            // Traverse at the *selected* version (prunes losing subtrees).
            final String version = selected.containsKey(frame.ga) ? //
                selected.get(frame.ga) : frame.requested;
            final State state = new State(frame.ga, version, frame.exclusions);
            if (!visited.add(state)) continue;

            final Release release = catalog.release(frame.ga, version);
            if (release == null || release.slim()) {
                continue; // leaf: unknown or recognition-only entry
            }

            // BOM-managed exclusions apply to this component's outgoing
            // edges wherever it appears (Maven depMgmt-exclusion semantics).
            Set<Exclusion> nodeExclusions = frame.exclusions;
            final ManagedEntry managed = catalog.managed().get(frame.ga);
            if (managed != null && !managed.exclusions().isEmpty()) {
                final Set<Exclusion> augmented = new HashSet<>(nodeExclusions);
                augmented.addAll(managed.exclusions());
                nodeExclusions = augmented;
            }

            for (final DependencyEdge edge : release.edges()) {
                if (edge.optional()) {
                    continue; // optional deps of dependencies are not traversed
                }
                if (excluded(edge.ga(), nodeExclusions)) {
                    continue; // pruned on this path
                }
                request(requests, edge.ga(), edge.version());
                Set<Exclusion> childExclusions = nodeExclusions;
                if (!edge.exclusions().isEmpty()) {
                    final Set<Exclusion> augmented = new HashSet<>(nodeExclusions);
                    augmented.addAll(edge.exclusions());
                    childExclusions = augmented;
                }
                stack.push(new Frame(edge.ga(), edge.version(), childExclusions));
            }
        }
        return requests;
    }

    private static void request(final Map<GA, Set<String>> requests,
        final GA ga, final String version)
    {
        Set<String> versions = requests.get(ga);
        if (versions == null) {
            versions = new HashSet<>();
            requests.put(ga, versions);
        }
        versions.add(version);
    }

    private static boolean excluded(final GA ga,
        final Set<Exclusion> exclusions)
    {
        for (final Exclusion exclusion : exclusions) {
            if (exclusion.matches(ga)) return true;
        }
        return false;
    }

    // -- Warnings --

    private static String highest(final Collection<String> versions) {
        String best = null;
        for (final String version : versions) {
            if (best == null || MavenVersion.compare(version, best) > 0) {
                best = version;
            }
        }
        return best;
    }

    private static Integer majorOf(final String version) {
        final Matcher m = MAJOR.matcher(version);
        return m.find() ? Integer.valueOf(m.group(1)) : null;
    }

    private static List<MediationWarning> warnings(
        final ComponentCatalog catalog, final Map<GA, String> selected,
        final Map<GA, Set<String>> requests)
    {
        final Set<MediationWarning> found = new LinkedHashSet<>();
        for (final Map.Entry<GA, Set<String>> entry : requests.entrySet()) {
            final GA ga = entry.getKey();
            final Set<String> versions = entry.getValue();
            final String chosen = selected.get(ga);
            final String highest = highest(versions);
            if (MavenVersion.compare(chosen, highest) < 0) {
                found.add(new MediationWarning(WarningType.HELD_BELOW, ga));
            }
            final Integer chosenMajor = majorOf(chosen);
            if (chosenMajor != null) {
                for (final String version : versions) {
                    final Integer major = majorOf(version);
                    if (major != null && !major.equals(chosenMajor)) {
                        found.add(
                            new MediationWarning(WarningType.MAJOR_MISALIGNMENT, ga));
                        break;
                    }
                }
            }
            if (catalog.release(ga, chosen) == null) {
                found.add(new MediationWarning(WarningType.MISSING_RELEASE, ga));
            }
        }
        final List<MediationWarning> sorted = new ArrayList<>(found);
        Collections.sort(sorted, new Comparator<MediationWarning>() {

            @Override
            public int compare(final MediationWarning a,
                final MediationWarning b)
            {
                final int byType = a.type().label().compareTo(b.type().label());
                if (byType != 0) return byType;
                return a.ga().compareTo(b.ga());
            }
        });
        return sorted;
    }
}

/*
 * #%L
 * ImageJ software for multidimensional image processing and analysis.
 * %%
 * Copyright (C) 2009 - 2025 Board of Regents of the University of
 * Wisconsin-Madison, Broad Institute of MIT and Harvard, and Max Planck
 * Institute of Molecular Cell Biology and Genetics.

 *

 * AND ANY EXPRESS OR IMPLIED WARRANTIES, INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, THE
 * IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE
 * ARE DISCLAIMED. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE COPYRIGHT HOLDERS OR CONTRIBUTORS BE
 * LIABLE FOR ANY DIRECT, INDIRECT, INCIDENTAL, SPECIAL, EXEMPLARY, OR
 * CONSEQUENTIAL DAMAGES (INCLUDING, BUT NOT LIMITED TO, PROCUREMENT OF
 * SUBSTITUTE GOODS OR SERVICES; LOSS OF USE, DATA, OR PROFITS; OR BUSINESS
 * INTERRUPTION) HOWEVER CAUSED AND ON ANY THEORY OF LIABILITY, WHETHER IN
 * CONTRACT, STRICT LIABILITY, OR TORT (INCLUDING NEGLIGENCE OR OTHERWISE)
 * ARISING IN ANY WAY OUT OF THE USE OF THIS SOFTWARE, EVEN IF ADVISED OF THE
 * POSSIBILITY OF SUCH DAMAGE.
 * #L%
 */

package net.imagej.updater.maven;

import java.io.IOException;
import java.io.InputStream;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;

import javax.xml.parsers.DocumentBuilderFactory;
import javax.xml.parsers.ParserConfigurationException;

import org.w3c.dom.Document;
import org.w3c.dom.Element;
import org.w3c.dom.Node;
import org.w3c.dom.NodeList;
import org.xml.sax.SAXException;

/**
 * Reads {@code <component>} and {@code <managed>} blocks of the
 * Maven-native index format (doc/maven-native-update-sites.md §3) into a
 * {@link ComponentCatalog}.
 * <p>
 * These elements coexist with legacy {@code <plugin>} entries in
 * {@code db.xml.gz}; legacy elements are ignored here and continue to be
 * handled by {@link net.imagej.updater.XMLFileReader}.
 * </p>
 */
public final class IndexXmlReader {

    private IndexXmlReader() {}

    /** Parses a whole index document from a stream. */
    public static ComponentCatalog parse(final InputStream in)
        throws IOException
    {
        try {
            final DocumentBuilderFactory factory =
                DocumentBuilderFactory.newInstance();
            final Document document = factory.newDocumentBuilder().parse(in);
            return parseCatalog(document.getDocumentElement());
        }
        catch (final ParserConfigurationException | SAXException exc) {
            throw new IOException(exc);
        }
    }

    /**
     * Parses {@code <component>} and {@code <managed>} children of the
     * given element into a catalog.
     */
    public static ComponentCatalog parseCatalog(final Element parent) {
        final ComponentCatalog catalog = new ComponentCatalog();
        for (final Element el : children(parent, "component")) {
            final MavenComponent component = parseComponent(el);
            catalog.components().put(component.ga(), component);
        }
        for (final Element el : children(parent, "managed")) {
            parseManaged(el, catalog);
        }
        return catalog;
    }

    public static MavenComponent parseComponent(final Element el) {
        final MavenComponent component =
            new MavenComponent(GA.parse(el.getAttribute("coordinate")));
        component.setOffered("true".equals(el.getAttribute("offered")));
        if (!el.getAttribute("current").isEmpty()) {
            component.setCurrent(el.getAttribute("current"));
        }
        for (final Element relEl : children(el, "release")) {
            final Release release = new Release(relEl.getAttribute("version"));
            if (!relEl.getAttribute("sha1").isEmpty()) {
                release.setSha1(relEl.getAttribute("sha1"));
            }
            if (!relEl.getAttribute("timestamp").isEmpty()) {
                release.setTimestamp(relEl.getAttribute("timestamp"));
            }
            if (!relEl.getAttribute("filesize").isEmpty()) {
                release.setFilesize(Long.parseLong(relEl.getAttribute("filesize")));
            }
            if (!relEl.getAttribute("min-java").isEmpty()) {
                release.setMinJava(Integer.parseInt(relEl.getAttribute("min-java")));
            }
            release.setSlim("true".equals(relEl.getAttribute("slim")));
            for (final Element descEl : children(relEl, "description")) {
                release.setDescription(descEl.getTextContent());
            }
            for (final Element depEl : children(relEl, "dependency")) {
                release.edges().add(parseDependency(depEl));
            }
            component.add(release);
        }
        return component;
    }

    private static DependencyEdge parseDependency(final Element depEl) {
        final Coordinate coordinate =
            Coordinate.parse(depEl.getAttribute("coordinate"));
        GA ga = coordinate.ga();
        final String classifier = depEl.getAttribute("classifier");
        final String packaging = depEl.getAttribute("packaging");
        if (!classifier.isEmpty() || !packaging.isEmpty()) {
            ga = new GA(ga.group(), ga.artifact(), classifier, packaging);
        }
        final List<Exclusion> exclusions = new ArrayList<>();
        for (final Element exclEl : children(depEl, "exclude")) {
            exclusions.add(Exclusion.parse(exclEl.getTextContent().trim()));
        }
        final String platform = depEl.getAttribute("platform");
        return new DependencyEdge(ga, coordinate.version(), exclusions,
            platform.isEmpty() ? null : platform,
            "true".equals(depEl.getAttribute("optional")));
    }

    private static void parseManaged(final Element el,
        final ComponentCatalog catalog)
    {
        if (!el.getAttribute("bom").isEmpty()) {
            catalog.setBom(el.getAttribute("bom"));
        }
        for (final Element vEl : children(el, "version")) {
            final Coordinate coordinate =
                Coordinate.parse(vEl.getAttribute("coordinate"));
            final List<Exclusion> exclusions = new ArrayList<>();
            for (final Element exclEl : children(vEl, "exclude")) {
                exclusions.add(Exclusion.parse(exclEl.getTextContent().trim()));
            }
            catalog.managed().put(coordinate.ga(),
                new ManagedEntry(coordinate.ga(), coordinate.version(), exclusions));
        }
    }

    /** Direct child elements with the given tag name (non-recursive). */
    public static List<Element> children(final Element parent,
        final String tag)
    {
        final List<Element> result = new ArrayList<>();
        final NodeList nodes = parent.getChildNodes();
        for (int i = 0; i < nodes.getLength(); i++) {
            final Node node = nodes.item(i);
            if (node.getNodeType() == Node.ELEMENT_NODE &&
                tag.equals(node.getNodeName()))
            {
                result.add((Element) node);
            }
        }
        return result;
    }
}

package net.imagej.updater.maven;

import static org.junit.Assert.assertEquals;
import static org.junit.Assert.assertTrue;

import java.io.BufferedReader;
import java.io.IOException;
import java.io.InputStream;
import java.io.InputStreamReader;
import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;

import org.junit.Test;

/**
 * Validates {@link MavenVersion} ordering against the shared corpus
 * ({@code vectors/version-order.txt}), which the Python reference
 * implementation (via jgo's comparator) must order identically.
 */
public class MavenVersionTest {

    @Test
    public void testCorpusOrdering() throws IOException {
        final List<List<String>> groups = loadCorpus();
        assertTrue("corpus suspiciously small", groups.size() > 10);
        for (final List<String> group : groups) {
            final String first = group.get(0);
            for (final String other : group.subList(1, group.size())) {
                assertEquals(first + " != " + other, 0,
                    MavenVersion.compare(first, other));
            }
        }
        for (int i = 0; i < groups.size() - 1; i++) {
            for (final String a : groups.get(i)) {
                for (final String b : groups.get(i + 1)) {
                    assertTrue("expected " + a + " < " + b,
                        MavenVersion.compare(a, b) < 0);
                    assertTrue("expected " + b + " > " + a,
                        MavenVersion.compare(b, a) > 0);
                }
            }
        }
    }

    private List<List<String>> loadCorpus() throws IOException {
        final InputStream in =
            getClass().getResourceAsStream("vectors/version-order.txt");
        assertTrue("version-order.txt resource missing", in != null);
        final List<List<String>> groups = new ArrayList<>();
        try (final BufferedReader reader =
            new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)))
        {
            String line;
            while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
                line = line.trim();
                if (line.isEmpty() || line.startsWith("#")) continue;
                if (line.startsWith("=")) {
                    groups.get(groups.size() - 1).add(line.substring(1));
                }
                else {
                    final List<String> group = new ArrayList<>();
                    group.add(line);
                    groups.add(group);
                }
            }
        }
        return groups;
    }
}

/*

 * %%

 * ARE DISCLAIMED. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE COPYRIGHT HOLDERS OR CONTRIBUTORS BE

 * SUBSTITUTE GOODS OR SERVICES; LOSS OF USE, DATA, OR PROFITS; OR BUSINESS
 * INTERRUPTION) HOWEVER CAUSED AND ON ANY THEORY OF LIABILITY, WHETHER IN
 * CONTRACT, STRICT LIABILITY, OR TORT (INCLUDING NEGLIGENCE OR OTHERWISE)
 * ARISING IN ANY WAY OUT OF THE USE OF THIS SOFTWARE, EVEN IF ADVISED OF THE
 * POSSIBILITY OF SUCH DAMAGE.

package net.imagej.updater.maven;

import static org.junit.Assert.assertEquals;
import static org.junit.Assert.assertTrue;

import java.io.BufferedReader;
import java.io.IOException;
import java.io.InputStream;
import java.io.InputStreamReader;
import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.Collections;
import java.util.HashMap;
import java.util.HashSet;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.Set;
import java.util.TreeMap;
import java.util.TreeSet;

import javax.xml.parsers.DocumentBuilderFactory;

import net.imagej.updater.maven.MVSResolver.MediationWarning;
import net.imagej.updater.maven.MVSResolver.Resolution;
import net.imagej.updater.maven.MVSResolver.Root;
import net.imagej.updater.maven.MVSResolver.WarningType;

import org.junit.Test;
import org.w3c.dom.Document;
import org.w3c.dom.Element;

/**
 * Runs every golden mediation vector (shared with the db-xml-maven
 * reference implementation) through {@link MVSResolver}, in declared root
 * order and reversed — results must be enable-order independent.
 */
public class MVSResolverTest {

    @Test
    public void testAllVectors() throws Exception {
        final List<String> names = vectorNames();
        assertTrue("no vectors found", names.size() >= 14);
        for (final String name : names) {
            runVector(name, false);
            runVector(name, true);
        }
    }

    private void runVector(final String name, final boolean reverseRoots)
        throws Exception
    {
        final InputStream in =
            getClass().getResourceAsStream("vectors/" + name);
        assertTrue("missing vector resource: " + name, in != null);
        final Document document = DocumentBuilderFactory.newInstance()
            .newDocumentBuilder().parse(in);
        final Element vectorEl = document.getDocumentElement();
        assertEquals("mediation-vector", vectorEl.getNodeName());

        final Element catalogEl =
            IndexXmlReader.children(vectorEl, "catalog").get(0);
        final ComponentCatalog catalog = IndexXmlReader.parseCatalog(catalogEl);

        final Element rootsEl = IndexXmlReader.children(vectorEl, "roots").get(0);
        final List<Root> roots = new ArrayList<>();
        for (final Element rootEl : IndexXmlReader.children(rootsEl, "root")) {
            final Coordinate coordinate =
                Coordinate.parse(rootEl.getAttribute("coordinate"));
            roots.add(new Root(coordinate.ga(), coordinate.version(),
                "true".equals(rootEl.getAttribute("pinned"))));
        }
        final List<Exclusion> rootExclusions = new ArrayList<>();
        for (final Element exclEl : IndexXmlReader.children(rootsEl, "exclude")) {
            rootExclusions.add(Exclusion.parse(exclEl.getTextContent().trim()));
        }
        if (reverseRoots) Collections.reverse(roots);

        final Element expectedEl =
            IndexXmlReader.children(vectorEl, "expected").get(0);
        final Map<GA, String> expectedSelected = new HashMap<>();
        for (final Element selEl : IndexXmlReader.children(expectedEl, "select")) {
            final Coordinate coordinate =
                Coordinate.parse(selEl.getAttribute("coordinate"));
            expectedSelected.put(coordinate.ga(), coordinate.version());
        }
        final Set<MediationWarning> expectedWarnings = new HashSet<>();
        for (final Element warnEl : IndexXmlReader.children(expectedEl, "warning")) {
            expectedWarnings.add(new MediationWarning(
                WarningType.fromLabel(warnEl.getAttribute("type")),
                GA.parse(warnEl.getAttribute("coordinate"))));
        }

        final Resolution resolution =
            new MVSResolver().resolve(catalog, roots, rootExclusions);

        final String label = name + (reverseRoots ? " (reversed roots)" : "");
        assertEquals(label + ": selection mismatch", //
            readable(expectedSelected), readable(resolution.selected()));
        assertEquals(label + ": warnings mismatch", //
            readable(expectedWarnings),
            readable(new HashSet<>(resolution.warnings())));
    }

    private Map<String, String> readable(final Map<GA, String> selected) {
        final Map<String, String> result = new TreeMap<>();
        for (final Map.Entry<GA, String> entry : selected.entrySet()) {
            result.put(entry.getKey().toString(), entry.getValue());
        }
        return result;
    }

    private Set<String> readable(final Set<MediationWarning> warnings) {
        final Set<String> result = new TreeSet<>();
        for (final MediationWarning warning : warnings) {
            result.add(warning.toString());
        }
        return result;
    }

    private List<String> vectorNames() throws IOException {
        final InputStream in =
            getClass().getResourceAsStream("vectors/index.txt");
        assertTrue("vectors/index.txt resource missing", in != null);
        final List<String> names = new ArrayList<>();
        try (final BufferedReader reader =
            new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(in, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)))
        {
            String line;
            while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
                line = line.trim();
                if (!line.isEmpty()) names.add(line);
            }
        }
        return names;
    }
}

    def md5(self) -> str:
        """Compute the MD5 hash of the artifact."""

        """Compute the SHA1 hash of the artifact."""

    def _checksum(self, suffix, func):
        p = self.resolve()
        checksum_path = p.parent / f"{p.name}.{suffix}"
        return text(checksum_path) or func(binary(p)).hexdigest()

    def last_modified(self) -> datetime | None:
        """
        Return the deploy timestamp of this artifact on the remote repository,
        or ``None`` if it cannot be determined.

        This performs an HTTP HEAD request against each configured remote
        repository in order. For Nexus 3 repositories, the ``Last-Modified``
        HTTP header is unreliable (it reflects the *migration* date rather than
        the original deploy date when a repository was migrated from Nexus 2),
        so the Nexus REST API is queried instead. For all other repositories
        the ``Last-Modified`` header is used directly. The artifact is never
        downloaded.

        Note: this is the time the artifact was *deployed to the remote*, which
        is distinct from the local cache mtime. If you need the latter (really
        the time the file was cached locally, not when it was released), use:

            datetime.fromtimestamp(os.path.getmtime(artifact.resolve()))
        """
        for repo_url in self.context.remote_repos.values():

Write /home/curtis/code/ctrueden/db-xml-maven/src/dbxm/flatten.py 
"""
The jgo-based "flattener": seed Maven project(s) -> component index.

Per imagej-updater's doc/maven-native-update-sites.md §3/§5: model
building (POM inheritance, interpolation, profile activation) runs here,
once, at publish time; each emitted <release> carries only the
component's *direct* dependency edges, so the client can mediate the
composed graph (dbxm.mvs / MVSResolver).

Design points implemented:

- Closure obligation: edge lists are emitted for the transitive closure
  of everything offered, at every full-entry version mentioned.
- Per-platform expansion: profiles are activated under each supported
  platform's simulated OS properties; edges present on only some
  platforms get one element per platform with a platform attribute.
- Slim-entry fallback: versions whose POMs cannot be resolved degrade to
  recognition-only entries instead of failing the run.
- Checksums come from the repository's .sha1 sidecar files -- JARs are
  never downloaded, which is what keeps the back catalog cheap.
- Optional dependencies are omitted: our components are dependencies of
  the synthetic installation root, and Maven does not traverse optional
  dependencies of dependencies.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass

import requests
from jgo.maven import MavenContext, Model, ProfileConstraints

from .model import GA, Catalog, Component, Edge, Exclusion, Release

_log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

#: ImageJ Updater short platform names -> Maven (os.name, family, os.arch).
PLATFORMS: dict[str, tuple[str, str, str]] = {
    "linux64": ("Linux", "unix", "amd64"),
    "linux-arm64": ("Linux", "unix", "aarch64"),
    "macos64": ("Mac OS X", "mac", "x86_64"),
    "macos-arm64": ("Mac OS X", "mac", "aarch64"),
    "win64": ("Windows", "windows", "amd64"),
    "win-arm64": ("Windows", "windows", "aarch64"),
}

#: Dependency scopes that ship in an installation.
RUNTIME_SCOPES = ("compile", "runtime")

DEFAULT_REMOTE_REPOS = {
    "scijava.public": "https://maven.scijava.org/content/groups/public",
    "central": "https://repo1.maven.org/maven2",
}


def default_context(remote_repos: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> MavenContext:
    return MavenContext(remote_repos=remote_repos or dict(DEFAULT_REMOTE_REPOS))


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class _EdgeKey:
    ga: GA
    version: str
    exclusions: tuple[Exclusion, ...]


class Flattener:

    def __init__(
        self,
        context: MavenContext | None = None,
        platforms: dict[str, tuple[str, str, str]] | None = None,
        fetch_checksums: bool = True,
        fetch_filesizes: bool = False,
        timeout: int = 30,
    ):
        self.context = context or default_context()
        self.platforms = platforms or dict(PLATFORMS)
        self.fetch_checksums = fetch_checksums
        self.fetch_filesizes = fetch_filesizes
        self.timeout = timeout

    # -- Public API --

    def flatten(self, seeds: list[str], all_versions: bool = False) -> Catalog:
        """
        Build a catalog for the given seed coordinates ("g:a" or "g:a:v").

        Each seed becomes an offered component; when all_versions is True,
        every release version of the seed gets a full entry, otherwise only
        the current (given or newest release) version does. The transitive
        closure of every full entry is included.
        """
        catalog = Catalog()
        # Worklist of (GA, version) pairs still needing full entries.
        pending: list[tuple[GA, str]] = []
        done: set[tuple[GA, str]] = set()

        for seed in seeds:
            tokens = seed.split(":")
            if len(tokens) not in (2, 3):
                raise ValueError(f"Invalid seed (expected g:a or g:a:v): {seed}")
            ga = GA(tokens[0], tokens[1])
            project = self.context.project(ga.group, ga.artifact)
            current = tokens[2] if len(tokens) == 3 else project.release
            if not current:
                raise ValueError(f"No release version found for {ga}")
            component = catalog.component(ga)
            component.offered = True
            component.current = current
            versions = [current]
            if all_versions:
                versions = sorted(
                    {c.version for c in project.versions()} | {current}
                )
            for version in versions:
                pending.append((ga, version))

        while pending:
            key = pending.pop()
            if key in done:
                continue
            done.add(key)
            ga, version = key
            if catalog.release(ga, version) is not None:
                continue
            release = self._process(ga, version)
            catalog.component(ga).add(release)
            for edge in release.edges:
                pending.append((edge.ga, edge.version))

        return catalog

    # -- Internals --

    def _process(self, ga: GA, version: str) -> Release:
        """Build one component version's full (or slim) entry."""
        _log.info("Processing %s:%s", ga, version)
        component = self.context.project(ga.group, ga.artifact).at_version(version)

        try:
            pom = component.pom()
        except Exception as exc:
            _log.warning("Slim entry for %s:%s (POM unresolvable: %s)", ga, version, exc)
            return self._slim(ga, version)

        # Resolve direct dependencies under each platform's constraints.
        edge_platforms: dict[_EdgeKey, set[str]] = {}
        order: list[_EdgeKey] = []  # deterministic emission order
        for platform, (os_name, os_family, os_arch) in self.platforms.items():
            constraints = ProfileConstraints(
                os_name=os_name, os_family=os_family, os_arch=os_arch
            )
            try:
                model = Model(
                    pom, self.context, profile_constraints=constraints, lenient=True
                )
                deps, _ = model.dependencies(max_depth=1)
            except Exception as exc:
                _log.warning(
                    "Slim entry for %s:%s (model failed on %s: %s)",
                    ga, version, platform, exc,
                )
                return self._slim(ga, version)
            for dep in deps:
                if dep.scope not in RUNTIME_SCOPES or dep.optional:
                    continue
                key = _EdgeKey(
                    ga=GA(
                        dep.groupId,
                        dep.artifactId,
                        dep.classifier or "",
                        dep.type or "jar",
                    ),
                    version=dep.version,
                    exclusions=tuple(
                        sorted(
                            (Exclusion(p.groupId, p.artifactId) for p in dep.exclusions),
                            key=str,
                        )
                    ),
                )
                if key not in edge_platforms:
                    edge_platforms[key] = set()
                    order.append(key)
                edge_platforms[key].add(platform)

        all_platforms = set(self.platforms)
        edges: list[Edge] = []
        for key in order:
            present = edge_platforms[key]
            if present == all_platforms:
                edges.append(Edge(key.ga, key.version, key.exclusions))
            else:
                for platform in sorted(present):
                    edges.append(
                        Edge(key.ga, key.version, key.exclusions, platform=platform)
                    )

        release = Release(version=version, edges=edges, description=pom.description)
        artifact = component.artifact()
        if self.fetch_checksums:
            release.sha1 = self._sha1(artifact)
        if self.fetch_filesizes:
            release.filesize = self._filesize(artifact)
        return release

    def _slim(self, ga: GA, version: str) -> Release:
        release = Release(version=version, slim=True)
        if self.fetch_checksums:
            component = self.context.project(ga.group, ga.artifact).at_version(version)
            release.sha1 = self._sha1(component.artifact())
        return release

    def _artifact_urls(self, artifact) -> list[str]:
        prefix = str(artifact.component.path_prefix).replace("\\", "/")
        return [
            f"{repo}/{prefix}/{artifact.filename}"
            for repo in self.context.remote_repos.values()
        ]

    def _sha1(self, artifact) -> str | None:
        """
        SHA1 from the repository's .sha1 sidecar -- never downloads the JAR
        from remotes. Falls back to hashing a locally cached copy.
        """
        for url in self._artifact_urls(artifact):
            try:
                response = requests.get(f"{url}.sha1", timeout=self.timeout)
                if response.ok and response.text.strip():
                    return response.text.split()[0]
            except requests.RequestException:
                continue
        cached = artifact.cached_path
        if cached is not None and cached.exists():
            return hashlib.sha1(cached.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
        _log.warning("No sha1 available for %s", artifact)
        return None

    def _filesize(self, artifact) -> int | None:
        for url in self._artifact_urls(artifact):
            try:
                response = requests.head(url, timeout=self.timeout)
                length = response.headers.get("content-length")
                if response.ok and length:
                    return int(length)
            except requests.RequestException:
                continue
        return None

Write /home/curtis/code/ctrueden/db-xml-maven/src/dbxm/cli.py 
"""Command-line interface: `dbxm flatten <seeds...>`."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import logging
import sys
from importlib import metadata

from .flatten import DEFAULT_REMOTE_REPOS, Flattener, default_context
from .xmlio import write_index


def _generator() -> str:
    try:
        version = metadata.version("db-xml-maven")
    except metadata.PackageNotFoundError:
        version = "unknown"
    return f"db-xml-maven {version}"


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        prog="dbxm",
        description="Maven-native ImageJ update site tooling.",
    )
    subparsers = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)

    flatten = subparsers.add_parser(
        "flatten",
        help="Generate a component index from seed Maven coordinates.",
    )
    flatten.add_argument(
        "seeds",
        nargs="+",
        help="Seed coordinates: g:a (current release) or g:a:v (pinned).",
    )
    flatten.add_argument(
        "-o", "--output",
        help="Output file (default: index-<g>-<a>.xml for the first seed).",
    )
    flatten.add_argument(
        "--all-versions",
        action="store_true",
        help="Full back catalog: an entry for every release version of each seed.",
    )
    flatten.add_argument(
        "--repo",
        action="append",
        metavar="NAME=URL",
        help="Remote repository (repeatable; default: scijava.public + central).",
    )
    flatten.add_argument(
        "--no-checksums",
        action="store_true",
        help="Skip fetching .sha1 sidecar files (faster; for experiments).",
    )
    flatten.add_argument(
        "--filesizes",
        action="store_true",
        help="Also fetch artifact sizes via HTTP HEAD requests.",
    )
    flatten.add_argument("-v", "--verbose", action="store_true")

    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    logging.basicConfig(
        format="[%(levelname)s] %(message)s",
        level=logging.DEBUG if args.verbose else logging.INFO,
    )

    if args.command == "flatten":
        remote_repos = dict(DEFAULT_REMOTE_REPOS)
        for spec in args.repo or []:
            name, _, url = spec.partition("=")
            if not url:
                parser.error(f"--repo expects NAME=URL, got: {spec}")
            remote_repos[name] = url
        flattener = Flattener(
            context=default_context(remote_repos),
            fetch_checksums=not args.no_checksums,
            fetch_filesizes=args.filesizes,
        )
        catalog = flattener.flatten(args.seeds, all_versions=args.all_versions)
        output = args.output
        if not output:
            g, a = args.seeds[0].split(":")[:2]
            output = f"index-{g}-{a}.xml"
        write_index(catalog, output, _generator())
        components = len(catalog.components)
        releases = sum(len(c.releases) for c in catalog.components.values())
        print(f"Wrote {output}: {components} components, {releases} releases.")
        return 0

    return 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

Write /home/curtis/code/ctrueden/db-xml-maven/tests/test_xmlio.py 
"""Emission/parsing round-trip: a catalog survives write + re-read."""

from pathlib import Path
from xml.etree import ElementTree as ET

from dbxm.mvs import resolve
from dbxm.xmlio import emit_catalog, load_vector, parse_catalog

VECTORS_DIR = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "vectors"


def test_round_trip_all_vectors():
    for path in sorted(VECTORS_DIR.glob("*.xml")):
        vector = load_vector(path)
        emitted = emit_catalog(vector.catalog, generator="test")
        assert emitted.get("generator") == "test"
        assert emitted.get("format")
        reparsed = parse_catalog(emitted)

        # Structural equality via a second emission pass.
        assert ET.tostring(emit_catalog(reparsed, generator="test")) == ET.tostring(
            emitted
        ), f"{path.name}: catalog did not round-trip"

        # Behavioral equality: mediation results are identical.
        before = resolve(vector.catalog, vector.roots, vector.root_exclusions)
        after = resolve(reparsed, vector.roots, vector.root_exclusions)
        assert before.selected == after.selected
        assert set(before.warnings) == set(after.warnings)

Write /home/curtis/code/ctrueden/db-xml-maven/README.md 
# db-xml-maven: Maven-native ImageJ update site tooling

Server/CI-side tooling for the ImageJ Updater's Maven-native update site
design, specified in imagej-updater's
[`doc/maven-native-update-sites.md`](https://github.com/imagej/imagej-updater/blob/maven/doc/maven-native-update-sites.md)
(currently on that repository's `maven` branch).

> **History**: this repository originally prototyped a pure-Python Maven
> resolver (`maven.py`) plus a generator of fully pre-resolved
> `db.xml.gz` files, one update site per project version. Both halves
> are superseded: the resolver graduated into
> [jgo](https://github.com/apposed/jgo) v2+, and the pre-resolved-closure
> design was replaced because it does not compose across update sites.
> See the design doc for the full rationale.

## The two halves

### `dbxm.flatten` — the index flattener

Turns seed Maven coordinates into `<component>`/`<managed>` index blocks.
Model building (POM inheritance, property interpolation, per-platform
profile activation) runs here — once, at publish time, via jgo — and each
emitted `<release>` carries only the component's **direct** dependency
edges. The Updater client mediates the composed graph at update time.

```bash
# Current release of a project, with its full transitive closure:
uv run dbxm flatten sc.fiji:TrackMate

# Full back catalog (an entry for every release version of the seed):
uv run dbxm flatten org.scijava:parsington --all-versions
```

Properties worth knowing:

- **JARs are never downloaded.** Checksums come from the repository's
  `.sha1` sidecar files; only POMs are fetched. This is what makes full
  back catalogs cheap.
- **Per-platform expansion**: profiles are activated under each ImageJ
  Updater platform (linux64, linux-arm64, macos64, macos-arm64, win64,
  win-arm64); platform-specific edges get `platform` attributes.
- **Slim-entry fallback**: versions whose POMs cannot be resolved (ancient
  or malformed archaeology) degrade to recognition-only entries rather
  than failing the run.
- **Provenance stamps**: the emitted index records the generator name,
  version, and format version, so outputs are auditable and bulk
  regeneration after a generator fix is possible — the index is a
  materialized view of the POMs, never a second source of truth.

### `dbxm.mvs` — the MVS mediation reference implementation

The **normative reference** for the Updater's cross-site mediation
algorithm: Minimum Version Selection over the composed component graph,
with per-path exclusions, `pin > BOM > MVS` precedence, and non-blocking
warnings (`held-below`, `major-misalignment`, `missing-release`).

The Java client implementation (`net.imagej.updater.maven.MVSResolver`
in imagej-updater) must produce identical results. Both are validated
against the golden vectors in [`vectors/`](vectors/) — including with
roots in reversed order, since results must be independent of the order
in which update sites are enabled. The `vectors/` directory is copied
into imagej-updater's test resources; keep them in sync.

Maven *version ordering* is jgo's comparator (normative); the shared
corpus `vectors/version-order.txt` pins the subset both implementations
must agree on.

## Development

```bash
uv sync        # installs jgo from ../../apposed/jgo (editable) per pyproject
uv run pytest  # golden vectors, version corpus, emission round-trip
```

## Status

Working spike (Phase 0/1 of the design doc). Not yet done here:

- Merge-not-overwrite emission into an existing `db.xml.gz` alongside
  legacy `<plugin>` entries.
- `<managed>` BOM extraction from a seed's dependencyManagement
  (pom-scijava / sc.fiji:fiji), and min-java fields.
- Deploy timestamps (`Artifact.last_modified`) — currently omitted for
  speed.
- Validation gate diffing flattener output against `mvn dependency:list`.
- Eventual home: this tooling is destined for the imagej org once stable
  (repo name TBD), wired into list-of-update-sites automation.

Write /home/curtis/.claude/projects/-home-curtis-code-imagej-imagej-updater/memory/maven-native-update-sites.md 
---
name: maven-native-update-sites
description: Multi-repo project teaching the ImageJ Updater Maven-native update sites (MVS mediation + jgo flattener); design doc, branches, and sync obligations
metadata:
  type: project
---

Ongoing project (started 2026-07-03): make the ImageJ Updater Maven-native.
Design doc: `doc/maven-native-update-sites.md` on imagej-updater's **maven branch** (work happens there, not master).

Key decisions (settled with Curtis, logged in doc §8):
- Mediation = **MVS/highest-wins** (equivalent given concrete-versions-only edges), NOT Maven nearest-wins. Per-component facts match Maven exactly via jgo at publish time; "ship the facts, compute the choice."
- Precedence: user pin > BOM (`<managed>`) > MVS; warnings: held-below, major-misalignment, missing-release (never blocking).
- Client mediation in **Java** (`net.imagej.updater.maven` package); Fiji not ready to bundle Python.
- Cutover via a temporary test fork of the Fiji update site (https://sites.imagej.net/Fiji/); legacy non-Maven + historical-recognition entries kept indefinitely. list-of-update-sites automation (`source:` key in sites.yml) comes last.

Repo roles:
- `~/code/imagej/imagej-updater` (maven branch): Java client — MVSResolver, MavenVersion (ported from jgo), IndexXmlReader, vector tests.
- `~/code/ctrueden/db-xml-maven` (main): recast as `dbxm` Python package — normative MVS reference (`dbxm.mvs`), jgo-based flattener (`dbxm.flatten`, CLI `uv run dbxm flatten g:a`), golden vectors in `vectors/`. Old maven.py/updater.py deleted (superseded by jgo v2 at `~/code/apposed/jgo`).

Sync obligation: `vectors/*.xml` + `index.txt` + `version-order.txt` in db-xml-maven must be copied to imagej-updater `src/test/resources/net/imagej/updater/maven/vectors/` whenever changed. Both test suites must pass (Python: `uv run pytest`; Java: `mvn test`).

Remaining (see db-xml-maven README "Status" + doc §6): merge-into-db.xml.gz emission alongside legacy `<plugin>` entries, `<managed>` BOM extraction from seed depMgmt, min-java fields, timestamps, mvn validation gate, client integration with FilesCollection/UI, manifest+lockfile, test site fork.

Upstream findings to report to jgo eventually: unknown-qualifier ordering diverges from Maven's ComparableVersion (jgo ranks unknown < sp; Maven ranks unknown > sp), and the SemVer fast path can disagree with the Maven-spec path for unknown qualifiers (e.g. 1.0.0-abc). Version-order corpus deliberately avoids these cases.

Bash End-to-end: mediate TrackMate installation from generated index 
cd /home/curtis/code/ctrueden/db-xml-maven && gzip -k -9 -c /tmp/claude-1000/-home-curtis-code-imagej-imagej-updater/e8b2271f-24f1-43d1-b743-afdafe4e2ae1/scratchpad/index-trackmate.xml | wc -c && uv run python - <<'EOF'
from xml.etree import ElementTree as ET
from dbxm.xmlio import parse_catalog
from dbxm.mvs import resolve, Root
from dbxm.model import GA

root_el = ET.parse("/tmp/claude-1000/-home-curtis-code-imagej-imagej-updater/e8b2271f-24f1-43d1-b743-afdafe4e2ae1/scratchpad/index-trackmate.xml").getroot()
catalog = parse_catalog(root_el)

tm = GA("sc.fiji", "TrackMate")
current = catalog.components[tm].current
print(f"Root: {tm}:{current}, catalog: {len(catalog.components)} components")

res = resolve(catalog, [Root(tm, current)])
print(f"Mediated selection: {len(res.selected)} components")
print(f"Warnings: {[str(w) for w in res.warnings] or 'none'}")

# Spot-check a few well-known selections:
for name in ("net.imglib2:imglib2", "org.scijava:scijava-common", "net.imagej:ij"):
    ga = GA(*name.split(":"))
    print(f"  {ga} -> {res.selected.get(ga, 'ABSENT')}")

# Sanity: every selected version has a catalog entry (closure holds).
missing = [f"{ga}:{v}" for ga, v in res.selected.items() if catalog.release(ga, v) is None]
print(f"Selected versions missing from catalog: {missing or 'none'}")
EOF

Claude 2026-07-04 17:37:54

Phase 0 is built, tested, and committed in both repos. All six tasks are done.

What exists now

imagej-updater, maven branch (commits c87ea32, cf0f94b): the net.imagej.updater.maven package — the component/managed index data model, a DOM reader for the new index elements, Maven version ordering ported from jgo, and MVSResolver implementing the fixpoint with per-path exclusions, pin > BOM > MVS precedence, and the three warning types. The full suite passes: 63 tests, no regressions in the legacy updater tests.

db-xml-maven, main (commits f0e109e, 287b270): the repo is recast per the plan — old maven.py/updater.py deleted, replaced by the dbxm package containing the normative Python MVS reference, the 14 golden mediation vectors both implementations pass (in declared and reversed root order), a version-ordering corpus validated against jgo, and the jgo-based flattener with a dbxm flatten CLI. 31 Python tests green.

Smoke-test results

The end-to-end pipeline works on real data: dbxm flatten sc.fiji:TrackMate resolved the full closure — 230 components, 555 releases, 42 KB gzipped — and feeding that index back through the parser and mediating with TrackMate 8.1.6 as root selected 221 components with the closure property holding (every selected version has a catalog entry with edges). SHA1s all came from .sha1 sidecars; no JARs were downloaded. The back-catalog run on parsington handled real archaeology gracefully: a bogus pre-rename version string in its maven-metadata degraded to a slim recognition-only entry instead of failing.

Three findings worth your attention

  1. The major-misalignment warning is too noisy raw: 27 of 221 TrackMate components fire it, mostly annotation/logging libraries with habitual major churn (jsr305, guava, slf4j, asm) alongside genuinely meaningful ones (imglib2, imagej-common). I recorded this in the doc’s open question §8.6 — the UI will need severity tiers or a scope restriction (e.g., warn prominently only for roots’ direct edges).
  2. Two jgo divergences to report upstream (noted in memory, corpus avoids them): unknown qualifiers rank below sp in jgo but above it in Maven’s real ComparableVersion, and jgo’s SemVer fast path can disagree with its own Maven-spec path for unknown qualifiers.
  3. Vector sync obligation: the vectors live canonically in db-xml-maven and are copied into imagej-updater’s test resources — currently in sync, but worth automating in CI eventually.

Natural next steps, in rough order: merge-not-overwrite emission into an existing db.xml.gz alongside legacy <plugin> entries, <managed> BOM extraction from a seed’s dependencyManagement (needed before flattening sc.fiji:fiji), and then the Fiji test-site fork.

Claude 2026-07-04 17:45:44

Yes — proceed. Phase 1 is mostly freezing what already exists and works, so the highest-value thing right now is your review of the semantics before they harden, rather than more building from me. Here’s the split I’d suggest:

Worth your eyes before format freeze (Phase 1):

  1. The 14 golden vectors (db-xml-maven/vectors/) — they are the contract being frozen; each is a short readable XML file with a prose description. Three encode judgment calls you should explicitly bless:
    • unpinned-root-raised: an extension’s dependency can raise another site’s current version above what that site offers. Maven-consistent, but it’s a UX decision that a site’s “current” is a floor, not a fixed point.
    • prune-dropped-subgraph: composing extensions that want honey:1 and honey:2 selects 2 and warns — your original bee/stinger example now warns on honey, which is arguably correct but contributes to warning noise.
    • bom-exclusion: managed exclusions apply to the managed component’s subtree (Maven depMgmt semantics), not globally. I just fixed the design doc, which wrongly said “globally” — the implementations and vector were already right (commit 1537407).
  2. The warning-noise finding (doc §8.6): 27 misalignment warnings on TrackMate’s real graph. The heuristic needs a severity policy before it reaches UI, and that’s a taste call.
  3. One Phase 1 decision only you can make: the installation manifest format — the file recording (site, coordinate, pin) roots plus exclusions. TOML mirroring jgo.toml? XML next to db.xml.gz? Where it lives in Fiji.app? Everything else in Phase 1 is mechanical once that’s chosen.

Meanwhile I’ve kept building (both committed): --managed BOM extraction now works — dbxm flatten sc.fiji:fiji --managed sc.fiji:fiji pulls 667 curated entries out of the effective dependencyManagement (pom-scijava via the parent chain), with exclusions preserved. And the real Phase-0-exit test is running in the background right now: flattening sc.fiji:fiji itself with its BOM — this is the run that will finally exercise platform-tagged native edges (JOGL/JNA/etc.), which TrackMate never triggered. A monitor will report when it lands, and I’ll validate the output the same way as TrackMate (parse → mediate → closure check).

If you want a hands-on test yourself, uv run dbxm flatten sc.fiji:your-favorite-plugin in db-xml-maven is a one-liner and real-world outputs from projects you know intimately are the best bug-finders. After the fiji run validates, the natural Phase 2 build order on my side is: merge-not-overwrite emission into an existing db.xml.gz, then wiring MVSResolver into FilesCollection’s update-check flow behind the new schema elements.

      f"{sum(len(c.releases) for c in catalog.components.values())} releases; "
      f"managed {len(catalog.managed)} (bom={catalog.bom})")

counts = collections.Counter(w.type for w in res.warnings)
print(f"Warnings: {dict(counts)}")

Read /home/curtis/code/ctrueden/db-xml-maven/src/dbxm/flatten.py 
{
  "offset": 88,
  "limit": 55
}
88           """
89          Build a catalog for the given seed coordinates ("g:a" or "g:a:v").
90  
91          Each seed becomes an offered component; when all_versions is True,
92          every release version of the seed gets a full entry, otherwise only
93          the current (given or newest release) version does. The transitive
94          closure of every full entry is included.
95          """
96          catalog = Catalog()
97          # Worklist of (GA, version) pairs still needing full entries.
98          pending: list[tuple[GA, str]] = []
99          done: set[tuple[GA, str]] = set()
100 
101         for seed in seeds:
102             tokens = seed.split(":")
103             if len(tokens) not in (2, 3):
104                 raise ValueError(f"Invalid seed (expected g:a or g:a:v): {seed}")
105             ga = GA(tokens[0], tokens[1])
106             project = self.context.project(ga.group, ga.artifact)
107             current = tokens[2] if len(tokens) == 3 else project.release
108             if not current:
109                 raise ValueError(f"No release version found for {ga}")
110             component = catalog.component(ga)
111             component.offered = True
112             component.current = current
113             versions = [current]
114             if all_versions:
115                 versions = sorted(
116                     {c.version for c in project.versions()} | {current}
117                 )
118             for version in versions:
119                 pending.append((ga, version))
120 
121         while pending:
122             key = pending.pop()
123             if key in done:
124                 continue
125             done.add(key)
126             ga, version = key
127             if catalog.release(ga, version) is not None:
128                 continue
129             release = self._process(ga, version)
130             catalog.component(ga).add(release)
131             for edge in release.edges:
132                 pending.append((edge.ga, edge.version))
133 
134         return catalog
135

You 2026-07-04 20:23:05

Could you explain to me why we need <managed> in the new schema? Don’t versions all get resolved on the server-side by dbxm via jgo? So we wend up with only <component> entries with direct <dependency> declarations at concrete versions? And then MVS can operate on that on the client side? Where does <managed> come into play?

Claude 2026-07-04 20:24:06

Good challenge — and you’re right about the mechanics: every <component> edge already has dependencyManagement baked in… but only its own. TrackMate 8.1.6’s edges reflect the pom-scijava that TrackMate was built against. What <managed> carries is something no per-component entry can: the root project’s depMgmt — the installation-level curation — and that can’t be resolved server-side because the thing it applies to (the composed set of extensions the user enabled) doesn’t exist until compose time on the client.

Recall the synthetic-root model: the installation behaves like a Maven project depending on all selected extensions. In Maven, that root project’s dependencyManagement overrides transitive versions everywhere. That’s exactly how sc.fiji:fiji works as a POM today — pom-scijava’s managed versions govern the whole app — and it’s what pombast melt validates. <managed> is that same fact shipped to the client so composition behaves like the build.

What you lose without it, concretely — all three showed up in yesterday’s fiji validation:

  1. MVS can only go up; curation sometimes needs to hold down. Highest-requested-wins has no mechanism to keep a library below what some POM asks for. The fiji run produced 6 held-below warnings — cases where a component’s POM requests a newer version than pom-scijava pins, and the BOM holds the line at the version the mega-melt actually validated. Without <managed>, any one plugin whose POM requests guava 34.x drags guava up for the entire installation, tested or not.

  2. Coherent selections vs. untested constellations. Different components were built against different pom-scijava generations, so their edges request a scatter of versions (that’s the source of the 70 misalignment warnings). Pure MVS picks the highest requested of each library independently — a combination that no one ever built or tested together. The BOM replaces that scatter with the one constellation pombast validated. It’s the difference between “a consistent selection” and “the tested selection.”

  3. Ecosystem-wide upgrades with stale third-party indexes. When pom-scijava 46 bumps imglib2, only the core site regenerates. Third-party sites’ published edges still request the old versions — and for libraries reachable only through those extensions (not in fiji’s own closure), MVS would leave them lagging while fiji’s own edges raise everything else. One <managed> block moves every reachable managed component to the new curated set in lockstep, without requiring every site on earth to republish. That’s how the core site retains stewardship over shared libraries across independently-published extension sites — the update-site equivalent of what pom-scijava does for builds.

There’s also a small factual payload that can’t live anywhere else: depMgmt entries carry exclusions that apply wherever the managed dependency appears (the guava→jsr305 pattern). Those are root-context facts — they can’t be baked into other components’ edges at publish time, because those components’ POMs don’t contain them.

And one thing our own bug made tangible: <managed> genuinely changes selections, not just warnings. The okio case — edges requested 3.2.0, BOM pins 3.9.1, mediation selects 3.9.1, a version no edge anywhere requested. That selection is pure <managed> at work (and it’s why the flattener now takes the closure under BOM overrides).

So the clean way to state the division of labor: publish time resolves all the facts (what each component, in its own context, directly needs); <managed> is not a fact about any component — it’s the installation’s preference, applied at the only moment a coherent installation exists, which is compose time. Could the system function without it? Mechanically yes — MVS alone still yields a consistent result — but you’d forfeit hold-below, tested-constellation coherence, and centrally-steered upgrades, which together are most of what makes Fiji Fiji rather than a pile of highest-versions. At 667 entries inside a 180 KB index, it’s cheap insurance on all three.

You 2026-07-04 21:19:14

MVS can only go up; curation sometimes needs to hold down.

Curation holding down feels like an anti-pattern to me. If the user has enabled the core Fiji update site (sc.fiji:fiji), plus that of some 3rd party plugin – say, Sparkles – that is not managed in pom-scijava at all… my strong intuition is that plain MVS behavior across the composed set of extensions, with no extra BOM applied, is the correct thing to do. Otherwise: which BOM(s) should we be applying to the composed installation: the Fiji update site BOM/managed block, or the Sparkles update site BOM/managed block, or both somehow? If Sparkles wants a newer version of guava than the one inherited by Fiji core, we should still ship that newer version, shouldn’t we? Otherwise, Sparkles would be broken. (The sensibility here is that upgrading a library is less likely to break things than downgrading a library.)

Coherent selections vs. untested constellations.

We are going to get untested constellations all the time – there are many update sites, many of which are tracking components not managed in pom-scijava. Sure, I can and do push to get as many of them managed in pom-scijava as possible, so that we can detect incompatibilities early via pombast. But it’s never going to be 100%, and update site maintainers are going to be frustrated when they release their plugin as a Maven component depending on e.g. guava 34 while Fiji still depends on guava 33, and no matter what they do, their users never receive guava 34. Doesn’t that feel wrong to you?

Ecosystem-wide upgrades with stale third-party indexes.

A big benefit of MVS resolution on the client side is that such stale indexes should not cause any problems. That is: if Fiji core needs a newer version of some library than Sparkles declares, users will receive the newer version. But if Sparkles needs a newer version, users will receive that one. If we need a new version of e.g. guava (which is not tracked by its own dedicated update site), it’s because some component somewhere (be it Fiji core itself, or some plugin, or multiple plugins) need it. So the workflow is: bump guava in pom-scijava, release new pom-scijava, bump pom-scijava in component(s) needing the newer guava, release that/those component(s), and then the relevant update site(s) will subsequently want that newer guava.

is not a fact about any component — it’s the installation’s preference

Let’s see if I comprehend your meaning here. Firstly: the design of Maven-based update sites is that an update site tracks a single Maven project (G:A) coordinate at the top level, right? So e.g. the core Fiji update site tracks sc.fiji:fiji. We aren’t going to support tracking some jgo-style composed endpoint with multiple coordinates, are we?

Assuming it’s one toplevel Maven project coordinate per update site: we then have a single coherent <dependencyManagement> section for that Maven component (the project at its most recent version, or perhaps a declared pinned version). Is that the <dependencyManagement> section we are then translating into a <managed> block?

Assuming yes: that implies one <managed> block per update site, yes? So then the question becomes: at client-side dependency mediation time, how do we reconcile the multiple <managed> blocks? In the case of Fiji plus Sparkles: which <managed> section “wins”? Or do we compose them somehow? What if Fiji’s pom-scijava 45

Suppose that: - we are composing sc.fiji:fiji:2.18.0 plus org.shiny:sparkles:1.0.0; - fiji 2.18.0 extends pom-scijava 45.0.0, which manages scripting-renjin at 0.2.3 and excludes org.apache.commons:commons-math; - sparkles 1.0.0 extends pom-scijava 39.0.0, which does not. Do we exclude the commons-math library from the composed dependency set, or not?

Claude 2026-07-04 21:21:46

You’ve convinced me — and your questions expose that <managed>-as-override isn’t just debatable, it’s incoherent with a decision we already made. When we chose MVS over Maven’s nearest-wins, we adopted Go’s composition philosophy; keeping the BOM as a selection override grafts Maven’s root-depMgmt semantics back onto it. The grafting point is exactly where your questions bite, because Maven’s model has one root with one depMgmt, and our composed installation has neither.

Taking your questions in order:

One G:A per site? Yes — that’s the design, via the source: key. The jgo-style composed endpoint does exist in the picture, but it’s the installation (the user’s manifest of enabled roots), not any site. Sites publish; the installation composes.

Is <managed> the seed’s effective depMgmt? Yes, exactly as implemented — which means one managed block per site, and your reconciliation question has no good answer. First-wins is order-dependent (and, embarrassingly, is what my Catalog.merge currently does — it directly violates the enable-order-independence invariant we test for; you found a real flaw). “Core site wins” requires the client to know which site is privileged, a concept that doesn’t otherwise exist. Merging by taking the highest managed version per GA makes managed entries behave like… requests — at which point they’re redundant with edges. Every reconciliation rule is either arbitrary or dissolves the feature. That’s the tell that the feature is wrong.

Your commons-math scenario, answered concretely under both semantics:

  • Current implementation: fiji’s managed entry for scripting-renjin (first-merged) attaches the commons-math exclusion to scripting-renjin’s outgoing edges on every path — including through sparkles’ subtree, whose pom-scijava-39 context never asked for it. Commons-math is excluded everywhere. Sparkles’ scripting-renjin usage silently loses a library it may need.
  • Pure MVS with per-path exclusions: fiji’s own edge to scripting-renjin already carries the exclusion (depMgmt exclusions merge into a component’s direct edges during its own model building — that fact is baked in at publish time and needs no managed block). Sparkles’ path to scripting-renjin carries no exclusion. Commons-math survives via the surviving path and ships. Someone still asks for it, in a context that didn’t exclude it, so it’s there.

The second answer is the principled one, and note the satisfying detail: the legitimate part of depMgmt exclusions (a component’s own curation of its own direct deps) is already captured in the published facts without <managed> at all. What the managed block added was only the power to retroactively rewrite other publishers’ subtrees — which is precisely the overreach you’re objecting to.

On your other two points, I concede with one honest cost to flag:

  • Guava 34 / Sparkles: agreed — under pure MVS the user gets guava 34, and the maintainer frustration you describe (“no matter what they do, users never receive it”) would have been a genuine adoption killer for third-party sites. The upgrade-over-downgrade bet is also Go’s empirical bet, and a decade of Go modules has borne it out.
  • The cost: without hold-down, the core site alone no longer reproduces the released Fiji constellation exactly. Our fiji run showed 6 held-below cases — components whose POMs request above pom-scijava’s pin (slf4j-style). Pure MVS gives core-only users those newer versions, slightly diverging from what mega-melt validated. I think the right posture is yours: that divergence is real version pressure being surfaced instead of silently overridden, and the fix is forward (pom-scijava catches up, or pombast learns to validate the MVS-selected constellation too). And for the genuinely-broken-version emergency, the principled future mechanism is Go’s retract analog — the component’s own publisher marks a bad version so MVS avoids it — not a central BOM holding everyone down. User pins remain for local emergencies.

What I’d salvage from <managed>: demote it to diagnostic metadata, because it neatly solves the other open problem — the 70-warning noise. Instead of raw major-misalignment, the client can warn where selection deviates from an enabled site’s curation, with attribution: “guava: 34.0 selected (requested by Sparkles); Fiji curates 33.4.8.” That’s meaningful, tiered, and actionable, and it uses the managed data without letting it touch selection. (If you’d rather drop the block entirely to keep the format lean, that’s defensible too — the same warning could be derived server-side into a diagnostic file — but keeping it as explicitly non-normative data is my mild preference.)

If you bless this, the concrete change list: doc §3/§4.1/§8 (precedence becomes pin > MVS, managed re-specified as diagnostic-only, decision-log entry); rewrite three vectors (bom-overrides, bom-exclusion, wildcard-exclusion-managed) to encode the new semantics; strip managed handling from both resolvers’ selection paths (they get simpler — the walk loses a branch); revert the closure-under-BOM pass in the flattener (no BOM-selected versions means those extra entries are unnecessary — pleasingly, the “bug” I fixed yesterday stops being a bug at all); and keep --managed emission as metadata. It’s a half-day of changes and every one of them deletes complexity. Say the word.

without hold-down, the core site alone no longer reproduces the released Fiji constellation exactly.

Why not? Won’t the core site alone exactly match the dependency set of a particular Fiji release? Assuming we are operating in jgo’s default/managed mode rather than “raw” mode (as explained in ~/code/apposed/jgo/docs/dependency-management.md: “By default, jgo works around this Maven limitation by adding all endpoints to the synthesized POM’s <dependencyManagement> section using import scope. This ensures that the versions of transitive dependencies match those that each endpoint was actually built with.”).

Our fiji run showed 6 held-below cases — components whose POMs request above pom-scijava’s pin (slf4j-style)

How can I peruse these cases?

Instead of raw major-misalignment, the client can warn where selection deviates from an enabled site’s curation, with attribution: “guava: 34.0 selected (requested by Sparkles); Fiji curates 33.4.8.”

Do we need <managed> for that? Something like this seems good to me: “guava version mismatch: 33.4.8 for Fiji vs 34.0 for Sparkles vs 31.0 for Fizzbuzz” – which we could report even without any <managed> metadata; this is just the version each update site would have applied, in cases where mediation had to choose between two different major versions.

For the 70-warning noise: I concur that we’d benefit from some sort of inclusion/exclusion rules for components that should vs shouldn’t warn about major version mismatches. More generally: it might be cool to somehow declare a boolean isBreaking(String groupId, String artifactId, String version1, String version2) function/strategy for each component – defaulting to SemVer, but overridable per project. There are certainly some 3rd party projects that break things between “minor” digits e.g. 5.5.x to 5.6.x.

Anyway, let’s proceed with those concrete changes you describe.

cd /home/curtis/code/ctrueden/db-xml-maven && uv run python - <<'EOF'
from xml.etree import ElementTree as ET
from dbxm.xmlio import parse_catalog
from dbxm.mvs import resolve, Root, HELD_BELOW
from dbxm.model import GA
from jgo.maven import compare_versions
from functools import cmp_to_key

root_el = ET.parse("/tmp/claude-1000/-home-curtis-code-imagej-imagej-updater/e8b2271f-24f1-43d1-b743-afdafe4e2ae1/scratchpad/index-fiji2.xml").getroot()
catalog = parse_catalog(root_el)
fiji = GA("sc.fiji", "fiji")
res = resolve(catalog, [Root(fiji, catalog.components[fiji].current)])
vkey = cmp_to_key(compare_versions)

print("=== held-below cases: BOM pin vs highest surviving request ===\n")
for w in res.warnings:
    if w.type != HELD_BELOW:
        continue
    ga = w.ga
    pinned = catalog.managed[ga].version
    requests = sorted(res.requests[ga], key=vkey)
    highest = requests[-1]
    # Attribute: which *selected* components' edges request the higher version?
    requesters = []
    for cga, cv in res.selected.items():
        rel = catalog.release(cga, cv)
        if rel is None:
            continue
        for e in rel.edges:
            if e.ga == ga and compare_versions(e.version, pinned) > 0:
                requesters.append(f"{cga}:{cv} -> {e.version}")
    print(f"{ga}")
    print(f"  pom-scijava pin : {pinned}")
    print(f"  requested       : {', '.join(requests)}")
    print(f"  pure MVS selects: {highest}")
    for r in sorted(requesters):
        print(f"    requested above pin by {r}")
    print()
