Write /home/curtis/code/ctrueden/db-xml-maven/src/dbxm/versions.py 
"""
Maven version ordering for mediation -- the Maven-spec path only.

jgo's compare_versions() has a SemVer fast path (used when both strings
are valid SemVer 1.x) that diverges from Maven's version-order
specification for prerelease identifiers with embedded numbers: SemVer
compares "beta-9" vs "beta-19" as ASCII strings (beta-9 > beta-19),
while Maven tokenizes to (beta, 9) vs (beta, 19) and orders numerically
(beta-9 < beta-19). This bit on real data: net.imglib2:imglib2-cache
versions 1.0.0-beta-9 vs 1.0.0-beta-19.

Mediation must follow Maven's ecosystem-actual ordering, so this module
compares via jgo's MavenVersion class (the faithful Maven-spec
implementation), never the fast path. The Java client's
net.imagej.updater.maven.MavenVersion implements the same algorithm;
vectors/version-order.txt pins the shared corpus.

TODO: report the fast-path divergence upstream to jgo.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from functools import cmp_to_key

from jgo.maven import MavenVersion


def compare_versions(v1: str, v2: str) -> int:
    """Maven version-order-spec comparison; negative/zero/positive."""
    a, b = MavenVersion(v1.strip()), MavenVersion(v2.strip())
    if a < b:
        return -1
    if b < a:
        return 1
    return 0


version_key = cmp_to_key(compare_versions)

Claude 2026-07-04 21:45:49

Bash Confirm the ordering divergence and the fix 
cd /home/curtis/code/ctrueden/db-xml-maven && uv run python -c "
from dbxm.versions import compare_versions
from jgo.maven import compare_versions as jgo_cmp
print('dbxm (Maven spec):', compare_versions('1.0.0-beta-9', '1.0.0-beta-19'))
print('jgo (fast path)  :', jgo_cmp('1.0.0-beta-9', '1.0.0-beta-19'))
"
dbxm (Maven spec): -1
jgo (fast path)  : 1

Whenever a pin or BOM entry holds a library *below* a version some\nsurviving edge requests, the client shows a visible warning, not silent\nsuccess.\n\n**SemVer misalignment warning**: when the selected version of a G:A has a\ndifferent major version than some surviving edge's requested version\n(e.g. an extension requires `imglib2:5.x` but `6.2.0` is selected), the\nclient surfaces a compatibility warning naming the requesting\nextension(s) and the versions involved. This is a heuristic (not every\nproject follows SemVer) but the pom-scijava ecosystem largely does; the\nwarning is informational and never blocks.",
  "new_string": "**Precedence: user pin > MVS.** There is no site-level BOM override\n(decision log \u00a78): per-site BOMs cannot be reconciled order-independently\nacross sites, and holding a library below what some component requests\nbreaks that component \u2014 upgrades break less than holds (Go's MVS bet).\nAn exclusion prunes transitive reachability but never removes an\nexplicitly selected root. Whenever a *pin* holds a library below a\nversion some surviving edge requests, the client shows a visible\nwarning, not silent success. For the genuinely-broken-version emergency,\nthe future mechanism is a Go-`retract`-style marker published by the\n*component's own publisher*, not central hold-down.\n\n**SemVer misalignment warning**: when the selected version of a G:A has a\ndifferent major version than some surviving edge's requested version\n(e.g. an extension requires `imglib2:5.x` but `6.2.0` is selected), the\nclient surfaces a compatibility warning naming the requesting\nextension(s) and the versions involved \u2014 attribution is per enabled\nsite: \"guava: 33.4.8 for Fiji vs 34.0 for Sparkles\", derivable from the\nwalk without any BOM metadata. This is a heuristic (not every project\nfollows SemVer) but the pom-scijava ecosystem largely does; the warning\nis informational and never blocks."
}

Bash Verify pure-MVS fiji mediation, commit db-xml-maven changes 
cd /home/curtis/code/ctrueden/db-xml-maven && uv run pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -2 && uv run python - <<'EOF'
from xml.etree import ElementTree as ET
from dbxm.xmlio import parse_catalog
from dbxm.mvs import resolve, Root
from dbxm.model import GA
import collections
root_el = ET.parse("/tmp/claude-1000/-home-curtis-code-imagej-imagej-updater/e8b2271f-24f1-43d1-b743-afdafe4e2ae1/scratchpad/index-fiji2.xml").getroot()
catalog = parse_catalog(root_el)
fiji = GA("sc.fiji", "fiji")
res = resolve(catalog, [Root(fiji, catalog.components[fiji].current)])
counts = collections.Counter(w.type for w in res.warnings)
missing = [1 for ga, v in res.selected.items() if catalog.release(ga, v) is None]
print(f"pure-MVS fiji installation: {len(res.selected)} components, warnings {dict(counts)}, closure violations {sum(missing)}")
for name in ("org.slf4j:slf4j-api", "ch.qos.logback:logback-core", "net.imglib2:imglib2-cache"):
    ga = GA(*name.split(":"))
    print(f"  {ga} -> {res.selected.get(ga)}")
EOF
git add -A && git commit -q -m "Remove <managed> BOM from mediation; fix version ordering

Two changes settled with Curtis (imagej-updater doc decision log 7-8):

1. No site-level BOM override. Per-site BOMs have no order-independent
   cross-site reconciliation, hold-down breaks the components that
   requested newer versions, and third-party sites must be able to ship
   newer libraries than the core curates. Precedence is now pin > MVS;
   the managed model/parse/emit/extract code and BOM vectors are gone,
   and the walk loses its managed-exclusion branch. A component's own
   depMgmt (versions and exclusions) remains baked into its published
   edges by model building.

2. Mediation version ordering now uses jgo's Maven-spec MavenVersion
   path via dbxm.versions, bypassing jgo compare_versions()'s SemVer
   fast path, which diverges from the Maven version-order spec on
   prerelease identifiers with embedded numbers -- found on real data:
   imglib2-cache 1.0.0-beta-9 vs 1.0.0-beta-19 (fast path picks beta-9
   as newer). Corpus updated with the regression pair; divergence to be
   reported upstream to jgo.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>" && git log --oneline -1

Claude 2026-07-04 21:52:49

All changes are in — both suites green (27 Python / full Java suite), committed as 20595ac (db-xml-maven) and c792840 (imagej-updater). Answers to your three questions, with what the data showed:

Why the core site alone doesn’t exactly match a Fiji release: jgo’s default managed mode imports the endpoint’s POM into the synthesized root’s <dependencyManagement> — that is hold-down, applied from fiji’s own depMgmt. Pure MVS is the analog of jgo’s raw mode. The delta between the two is exactly the held-below set, and the report above lists all of them with attribution. After removing the spurious one (see below), there are 5 real cases in fiji 2.17.0: bio-formats 8.2.0 requests slf4j 2.0.9 over the 1.7.36 pin, ome-common requests logback 1.3.15, the grpc stack requests gson 2.11.0, jline requests jna 5.15.0, and okio requests kotlin-stdlib 1.9.25. Under pure MVS those now win — and notably the slf4j case resolves coherently: MVS raises slf4j-api and logback together, landing on the slf4j-2-compatible logback 1.3.x line, which is a nice early sign the upgrade bet behaves. These are now recorded in the doc’s decision log as accepted, surfaced version pressure.

A bonus catch from generating your report: the sixth “held-below” case was fake — jgo’s compare_versions() SemVer fast path ranked imglib2-cache 1.0.0-beta-9 above 1.0.0-beta-19 (SemVer compares the prerelease identifier beta-9 vs beta-19 as ASCII; Maven tokenizes and compares 9 < 19 numerically). That’s a genuine jgo bug worth fixing upstream, and it would have made the Python reference and Java client disagree on live data. Mediation now uses jgo’s MavenVersion class directly (the faithful Maven-spec path) via a new dbxm.versions module, and the shared corpus gained the beta-9/beta-19 regression pair — which the Java side already ordered correctly.

Warning attribution without <managed>: you’re right, and it’s gone completely — model, parser, emitter, --managed flag, extract_managed, the two BOM vectors, and the walk’s managed-exclusion branch (the resolver got simpler, and yesterday’s “closure under BOM overrides” fix was reverted since it’s no longer needed). The doc now specifies per-site attribution derived from the walk itself (“guava: 33.4.8 for Fiji vs 34.0 for Sparkles”), and your isBreaking(g, a, v1, v2) pluggable-strategy idea is recorded in open question §8.6, including the unresolved question of where per-project overrides would live.

Final numbers under the new semantics: mediating fiji 2.17.0 pure-MVS gives 820 components, 0 closure violations, 67 major-misalignment warnings and nothing else — no held-below (only pins can produce those now), no missing-release.

Next up per the earlier plan: merge-not-overwrite emission into an existing db.xml.gz, then wiring MVSResolver into the client’s update-check flow.

You 2026-07-04 22:30:21

Why is any MVS happening at all with only one update site? I thought we were using jgo dep resolution on the dbxm side, writing out that dependency graph as <component> elements to XML. The dependency versions need to match exactly what you’d get if you ran e.g. jgo sc.fiji:fiji:2.18.0 from the CLI – that’s what users are going to expect from an update site purporting to serve Fiji 2.18.0. The MVS only comes into play when composing multiple update sites on the client side, I thought!

About jgo’s version comparison bug: yeah, we should fix that in jgo. It’s supposed to be matching Maven’s version comparator exactly, so 1.0.0-beta-9 should compare as older than 1.0.0-beta-19 as you say, right?

Claude 2026-07-04 22:33:03

You’ve caught a real gap between what I built and what users will expect — and closing it turns out to make the design better, not more complicated. Let me own the mismatch precisely, then propose the fix.

What I built: the index publishes context-free facts — each component version’s own declared direct deps, from its own POM. The client then always mediates, even for one site. But MVS over declared requests is the analog of jgo’s raw mode: bio-formats 8.2.0 declares slf4j 2.0.9, so a single-site Fiji client would select slf4j 2.0.9 — while jgo sc.fiji:fiji:2.18.0 (managed mode, fiji’s depMgmt applied) gives 1.7.36. Those 5 held-below cases are the raw-vs-managed delta. You’re right that a site purporting to serve Fiji 2.18.0 must serve the constellation jgo default-mode resolves — that’s the release, full stop.

The fix — publish the site’s resolved selection alongside the facts:

<component coordinate="sc.fiji:fiji" offered="true" current="2.18.0">
  <release version="2.18.0" sha1="...">
    <selection>   <!-- jgo managed-mode resolution of this release; a published lockfile -->
      <select coordinate="org.slf4j:slf4j-api:1.7.36"/>
      <select coordinate="ome:formats-gpl:8.2.0"/>
      ... (~800 entries, gzips to almost nothing)
    </selection>
  </release>
</component>

with the client semantics reworked so that versions come from selections; edges provide only reachability:

  • Each enabled root contributes its release’s <selection>.
  • winner(ga) = pin, else max over the selections of roots that reach ga.
  • Reachability = walk the winners’ facts-edges with per-path exclusions (pruning still works: when Sparkles raises honey to 2, Fiji’s honey:1 subtree isn’t traversed, so stinger drops even though Fiji’s selection lists it).

This gives exactly the behavior you described: one site enabled → the walk is the identity → you get precisely the jgo-resolved constellation, and MVS only does work when composing multiple sites (or honoring a pin). Some pleasant consequences fall out:

  • Your warning message is now literally the data model: “guava: 33.4.8 for Fiji vs 34.0 for Sparkles” is just comparing the two sites’ selections for that GA.
  • The flattener gets more faithful and simpler in the risky dimension: instead of me approximating resolution semantics per component, each offered release’s selection comes from running jgo’s actual resolver — fidelity to jgo sc.fiji:fiji:2.18.0 by construction, not by emulation.
  • There’s a publish-time invariant the generator can verify and fail loudly on: walking the selection’s own graph must reproduce exactly the selection’s domain. That’s a strong self-check on facts/exclusion handling.
  • The 5-case fidelity gap disappears from the decision log; no BOM element returns — a <selection> is not an override, it’s the site’s own state, scoped to its own release. Composition still has no hold-down: cross-site conflicts resolve upward via MVS, exactly as you argued.

The facts edges stay in the format (reachability needs per-version edge lists, and they remain the right shared, context-free primitive; their versions demote to diagnostic/attribution data). Back catalog: one selection per offered release — still POM-only work, still cheap.

On the jgo comparator: confirmed, 1.0.0-beta-9 must compare older than 1.0.0-beta-19. Maven tokenizes to (beta, 9) vs (beta, 19) and compares numerically; jgo’s SemVer 1.x fast path compares the prerelease identifier as one ASCII string and gets it backwards. Since jgo’s contract is “match Maven’s comparator exactly,” the fast path should simply be removed (it can’t be trusted to agree even where both grammars accept the string). While you’re in there, one more divergence I noticed reading _version.py: unknown qualifiers — jgo ranks them at priority 0 (tied with release, alphabetical), but Maven’s ComparableVersion ranks unknown qualifiers above sp. Both fixes belong in the same jgo patch, and dbxm’s corpus already carries the beta-9/beta-19 pair as a regression test; once jgo is fixed, dbxm.versions can go back to plain compare_versions.

If you bless the <selection> design, the change list is: schema + doc (§3/§4, decision-log entry superseding part of yesterday’s), flattener (resolve each offered release via jgo’s PythonResolver and emit the selection, plus the self-check invariant), both MVS implementations (selection-driven winners; the walk actually gets simpler since edge versions drop out of selection), and vectors (roots gain selections; add a single-site-identity vector encoding the bio-formats/slf4j case, and rework the cross-site ones). It’s a bigger bite than yesterday’s change but the same character — every piece moves complexity from emulation to reuse.

Write /home/curtis/code/ctrueden/db-xml-maven/src/dbxm/mvs.py 
"""
Selection-based Minimum Version Selection (MVS) -- normative reference.

This is the cross-site composition algorithm defined in imagej-updater's
doc/maven-native-update-sites.md §4. The Java client
(net.imagej.updater.maven.MVSResolver) must produce identical results;
both implementations are validated against the golden vectors in
vectors/*.xml.

Semantics summary:

- The installation is a synthetic root; user-selected components are its
  direct dependencies ("roots"), each at the site's current version or a
  user-pinned version.
- **Versions come from selections; edges provide reachability.** Each
  offered release publishes a <selection>: its resolved constellation
  (what `jgo g:a:v` resolves in managed mode). A root contributes, for
  every component its surviving paths reach, the version its selection
  ships. With a single site enabled, mediation is therefore the
  identity: the client materializes exactly the release's constellation.
- Cross-site composition is MVS over the contributions: per G:A(:C:P),
  the highest contributed version wins. For roots whose release lacks a
  selection entry for a reached component (legacy or hand-built
  indexes), the edge-declared versions along that root's surviving
  paths serve as the fallback contribution.
- Precedence: user pin > MVS. There is deliberately no site-level BOM
  override: cross-site curation-by-hold-down does not compose and
  violates order independence; a site's own curation is fully expressed
  by its selection.
- Reachability walks each component at its *winning* version, so losing
  subgraphs are pruned: a dependency listed in one site's selection is
  still dropped if no surviving path reaches it once winners are chosen
  (e.g. a dependency dropped between versions of its dependee).
- Exclusions are per-path: an edge's exclusion set applies to the entire
  subtree traversed through that edge. Root exclusions (user "never
  install") apply globally. Exclusions never remove an explicitly
  selected root.
- Fixpoint iteration: walk each root -> gather contributions -> select
  -> repeat until stable. An unpinned root itself participates: another
  site's selection can raise it, and its own contribution then follows
  the raised release.

Warnings (never blocking):

- held-below: a user pin holds a component below some surviving
  contribution.
- major-misalignment: the selected version's leading numeric ("major")
  differs from some surviving contribution's major. With a single site
  this cannot fire (each component has one contribution).
- missing-release: the selected version has no catalog entry (closure
  violation upstream, or a slim-only component was selected).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
import re

from .model import GA, Catalog, Exclusion
from .versions import compare_versions, version_key

#: Iteration cap for the fixpoint loop. Real dependency graphs converge in
#: a handful of iterations; the cap guards against pathological oscillation.
MAX_ITERATIONS = 1000

HELD_BELOW = "held-below"
MAJOR_MISALIGNMENT = "major-misalignment"
MISSING_RELEASE = "missing-release"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Root:
    """A user-selected component: site current version, or a pinned one."""

    ga: GA
    version: str
    pinned: bool = False


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Warning:
    type: str
    ga: GA

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return f"{self.type}({self.ga})"


@dataclass
class Resolution:
    #: Final selection: one version per reachable G:A.
    selected: dict[GA, str]
    #: Deduplicated, deterministically ordered warnings.
    warnings: list[Warning]
    #: Surviving contributions from the stable walk (per G:A).
    contributions: dict[GA, set[str]] = field(default_factory=dict)


def resolve(
    catalog: Catalog,
    roots: list[Root],
    root_exclusions: tuple[Exclusion, ...] = (),
) -> Resolution:
    pins = {r.ga: r.version for r in roots if r.pinned}
    selected: dict[GA, str] = {}
    contributions: dict[GA, set[str]] = {}

    for _ in range(MAX_ITERATIONS):
        contributions = {}
        for root in roots:
            # An unpinned root's effective release follows the fixpoint:
            # another site's selection may have raised it.
            root_version = selected.get(root.ga, root.version)
            release = catalog.release(root.ga, root_version)
            selection = release.selection if release is not None else {}
            reached, edge_requests = _walk_root(
                catalog, root.ga, root_version, selection,
                root_exclusions, selected,
            )
            for ga in reached:
                versions = contributions.setdefault(ga, set())
                if ga in selection:
                    versions.add(selection[ga])
                else:
                    versions.update(edge_requests.get(ga, ()))

        new_selected: dict[GA, str] = {}
        for ga, versions in contributions.items():
            if not versions:
                continue
            version = max(versions, key=version_key)
            if ga in pins:
                version = pins[ga]
            new_selected[ga] = version
        if new_selected == selected:
            break
        selected = new_selected
    else:
        raise RuntimeError(
            f"MVS did not converge within {MAX_ITERATIONS} iterations"
        )

    warnings = _warnings(catalog, selected, contributions)
    return Resolution(selected, warnings, contributions)


def _walk_root(
    catalog: Catalog,
    root_ga: GA,
    root_version: str,
    selection: dict[GA, str],
    root_exclusions: tuple[Exclusion, ...],
    selected: dict[GA, str],
) -> tuple[set[GA], dict[GA, set[str]]]:
    """
    One root's traversal, honoring the current global selection.

    Node versions resolve as: global winner, else this root's selection,
    else the edge-declared version. Returns the set of components reached
    via surviving paths, plus the edge-declared versions per component
    (the fallback contributions for selection-less roots).
    """
    reached: set[GA] = set()
    edge_requests: dict[GA, set[str]] = {root_ga: {root_version}}
    # (ga, edge-declared version, accumulated exclusions along this path)
    stack: list[tuple[GA, str, frozenset[Exclusion]]] = [
        (root_ga, root_version, frozenset(root_exclusions))
    ]
    # Visited keys include the exclusion set: the same node may be pruned
    # differently on different paths, and only surviving paths contribute.
    visited: set[tuple[GA, str, frozenset[Exclusion]]] = set()

    while stack:
        ga, declared, exclusions = stack.pop()
        reached.add(ga)
        version = selected.get(ga) or selection.get(ga) or declared
        state = (ga, version, exclusions)
        if state in visited:
            continue
        visited.add(state)

        release = catalog.release(ga, version)
        if release is None or release.slim:
            continue  # leaf: unknown or recognition-only entry

        for edge in release.edges:
            if edge.optional:
                continue  # optional deps of dependencies are not traversed
            if any(e.matches(edge.ga) for e in exclusions):
                continue  # pruned on this path
            edge_requests.setdefault(edge.ga, set()).add(edge.version)
            child_exclusions = exclusions
            if edge.exclusions:
                child_exclusions = exclusions | set(edge.exclusions)
            stack.append((edge.ga, edge.version, frozenset(child_exclusions)))

    return reached, edge_requests


def _major(version: str) -> int | None:
    m = re.match(r"(\d+)", version)
    return int(m.group(1)) if m else None


def _warnings(
    catalog: Catalog,
    selected: dict[GA, str],
    contributions: dict[GA, set[str]],
) -> list[Warning]:
    found: set[Warning] = set()
    for ga, versions in contributions.items():
        if not versions:
            continue
        chosen = selected[ga]
        highest = max(versions, key=version_key)
        if compare_versions(chosen, highest) < 0:
            found.add(Warning(HELD_BELOW, ga))
        chosen_major = _major(chosen)
        if chosen_major is not None and any(
            (m := _major(v)) is not None and m != chosen_major for v in versions
        ):
            found.add(Warning(MAJOR_MISALIGNMENT, ga))
        if catalog.release(ga, chosen) is None:
            found.add(Warning(MISSING_RELEASE, ga))
    return sorted(found, key=lambda w: (w.type, str(w.ga)))

There is deliberately no site-level\n * BOM override: cross-site curation-by-hold-down does not compose (whose\n * BOM wins?) and violates order independence; curation lives at build\n * time (pom-scijava/pombast) and in each component's own published\n * facts.</li>\n * <li>Exclusions are per-path; root exclusions are global.   "new_string": " * <ul>\n * <li>The installation is a synthetic root; user-selected components are\n * its direct dependencies (\"roots\").</li>\n * <li><b>Versions come from selections; edges provide reachability.</b>\n * Each offered release publishes its resolved constellation (what\n * {@code jgo g:a:v} resolves); a root contributes, for every component\n * its surviving paths reach, the version its selection ships. With one\n * site enabled, mediation is the identity on the release constellation.\n * Roots without selection coverage (legacy indexes) contribute their\n * edge-declared versions as fallback.</li>\n * <li>Cross-site composition selects the highest contribution per\n * {@link GA} (MVS). Precedence: user pin &gt; MVS; no site-level BOM\n * override.</li>\n * <li>Exclusions are per-path; root exclusions are global. Exclusions\n * never remove an explicitly selected root.</li>\n * <li>Reachability walks each component at its <em>winning</em> version,\n * so losing subtrees are pruned even when a selection lists them.</li>\n * </ul>\n */"

There is intentionally **no `<managed>` (BOM) element**. An earlier
draft carried each site's effective dependencyManagement as a
compose-time override; it was removed (decision log §8) because
per-site BOMs have no order-independent reconciliation across sites,
and curation-by-hold-down conflicts with the MVS philosophy (a
component that requests a newer library needs that library; upgrades
break less than holds). The legitimate residue of depMgmt is already
in the facts: a component's own managed versions and exclusions are
baked into its published edges during model building.

### 4.1 Mediation algorithm

**DECIDED (2026-07-03): Minimum Version Selection (MVS).** Per-component
facts match Maven's behavior exactly (jgo at publish time); cross-site
composition uses MVS.

Note that in this format MVS and Gradle-style highest-wins coincide:
published edges carry only concrete versions (ranges are banned by the
closure rules, §3), each treated as a minimum requirement, so
"maximum of the minima" and "highest requested" compute the same
fixpoint. The distinction would matter only if ranges or upper bounds
were ever admitted — one more reason they stay banned.

Rationale for MVS over Maven's nearest-wins:

- **Order-independent and deterministic** regardless of graph shape — no
  depth tracking, no declaration-order tie-breaks, no dependence on which
  site was enabled first. More robust for user-composed installations.
- **Simpler client code**: the mediation walk shrinks to a monotone
  fixpoint (select highest requested per G:A:C:P; traverse winners'
  edges; repeat until stable — terminates because selections only
  increase over a finite version set).
- Each component's *own* dependency facts are still exactly Maven's; only
  the cross-extension composition rule differs, and Fiji's runtime (flat
  classpath, one version per library) never matched Maven's build-time
  nearest-wins anyway.

**Exclusion interplay** (ours to define, since Go's MVS has no such
concept): the fixpoint walk accumulates per-path exclusion sets exactly
as in §4.2; a version is a candidate only if requested via a surviving
(non-excluded) path. Exclusion filtering interleaves with selection in
each fixpoint iteration.

**Precedence: user pin > MVS.** There is no site-level BOM override
(decision log §8): per-site BOMs cannot be reconciled order-independently
across sites, and holding a library below what some component requests
breaks that component — upgrades break less than holds (Go's MVS bet).
An exclusion prunes transitive reachability but never removes an
explicitly selected root. Whenever a *pin* holds a library below a
version some surviving edge requests, the client shows a visible
warning, not silent success. For the genuinely-broken-version emergency,
the future mechanism is a Go-`retract`-style marker published by the
*component's own publisher*, not central hold-down.

**SemVer misalignment warning**: when the selected version of a G:A has a
different major version than some surviving edge's requested version
(e.g. an extension requires `imglib2:5.x` but `6.2.0` is selected), the
client surfaces a compatibility warning naming the requesting
extension(s) and the versions involved — attribution is per enabled
site: "guava: 33.4.8 for Fiji vs 34.0 for Sparkles", derivable from the
walk without any BOM metadata. This is a heuristic (not every project
follows SemVer) but the pom-scijava ecosystem largely does; the warning
is informational and never blocks.

### 4.2 Algorithm sketch

Contributions per component = the versions the reaching\nroots' selections ship; the highest contribution wins. Consequences:\n\n- **One site enabled \u21d2 identity**: the client materializes exactly the\n  release constellation that `jgo g:a:v` resolves \u2014 what users expect\n  from a site purporting to serve that release. MVS does real work only\n  when composing multiple sites (or honoring pins).\n- **Selections are contributions, not install lists**: reachability\n  walks the *winners'* edges, so a component listed in one site's\n  selection is still pruned when composition raises its dependee past\n  the version that needed it (bee/stinger).\n- Selection-less roots (legacy or hand-built indexes) fall back to\n  contributing their edge-declared versions.\n\nNote that in this composition MVS and Gradle-style highest-wins\ncoincide: contributions are concrete versions (ranges are banned by the\nclosure rules, \u00a73), each treated as a minimum requirement, so \"maximum\nof the minima\" and \"highest contributed\" compute the same fixpoint. The\ndistinction would matter only if ranges or upper bounds were ever\nadmitted \u2014 one more reason they stay banned."

Claude 2026-07-04 22:51:58

Bash Read §4.2 sketch for update 
sed -n '/### 4.2 Algorithm sketch/,/### 4.3/p' doc/maven-native-update-sites.md
### 4.2 Algorithm sketch

1. Fetch enabled sites' indexes (one HTTP request per site, as today).
2. Roots = user-selected offered components (with any pins) + root
   exclusions (user "never install X" preferences).
3. Walk the union graph from the roots. Each path accumulates the union
   of exclusion sets along its edges; matching nodes are pruned on that
   path. Exclusion filtering interleaves with mediation (an excluded node
   is not a version candidate via that path).
4. Mediate versions per G:A:C:P; traverse only the winner's edge list.
5. Filter release candidates by `min-java` against the running JVM.
6. Diff the mediated selection against local state (filename+SHA1
   recognition; slim entries let ancient installs be identified rather
   than reported as unknown files).
7. Download Maven-born files directly from Maven repositories
   (scijava.public proxies Central), SHA1-verified. sites.imagej.net
   hosts only non-Maven files and indexes.

Implementation — **DECIDED (2026-07-03): Java, in imagej-updater.** The
MVS fixpoint plus Maven's `ComparableVersion` ordering is a few hundred
lines, validated by golden vectors (§7). Full model building stays out of
the client permanently. Fiji is not ready to bundle a Python runtime; if
that changes, delegating to embedded jgo can be revisited, but the Java
implementation proceeds regardless.

Roots = user-selected offered components (with any pins) + root\n   exclusions (user \"never install X\" preferences).\n3. Fixpoint: walk each root's graph, traversing every component at its\n   current *winning* version (falling back to the root's selection, then\n   the edge-declared version). Each path accumulates the union of\n   exclusion sets along its edges; matching nodes are pruned on that\n   path.\n4. Contributions per reached G:A:C:P = the reaching roots' selection\n   versions (edge-declared versions for selection-less roots). Winner =\n   pin, else highest contribution. Repeat from 3 until stable. An\n   unpinned root itself participates: another site's selection may raise\n   it, and its own contribution then follows the raised release.\n5. Filter release candidates by `min-java` against the running JVM."

Claude 2026-07-04 23:11:27

The self-check earned its keep on the first real run: the walk over-reaches — it wanders into subgraphs that jgo’s resolution pruned (via root-depMgmt exclusions, scope overrides, and host-platform filtering), like xalan, eventbus:1.4, and Windows-i586 natives, and then fallback-mediates them. The selection knows about that pruning implicitly (pruned components are absent), but the traversal wasn’t consulting it for membership, only for versions. The principled fix: an on-selection node’s edges may only reach the selection’s domain — a selection-bearing root’s authoritative statement is its selection, so it never contributes fallback edge versions either. Off-selection subtrees (winner-raised, pinned-to-non-offered) still traverse freely with last-resort fallback. Let me implement, plus the regression vector:

<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<mediation-vector name="selection-prunes-overreach">
  <description>
    A selection is also a membership statement: core's facts edge
    declares junk:1, but the site's resolution pruned it (e.g. via the
    seed's depMgmt exclusions, scope overrides, or platform filtering),
    so junk is absent from the selection. The on-selection walk must not
    follow edges outside the selection domain -- junk stays out, exactly
    as jgo resolved. Found on real data: the fiji index's identity
    self-check over-reached into xalan/eventbus subgraphs jgo had pruned.
  </description>
  <catalog>
    <component coordinate="app:fiji" offered="true" current="1">
      <release version="1">
        <selection>

Bash Verify single-site identity on real fiji index

from xml.etree import ElementTree as ET
from dbxm.xmlio import parse_catalog
from dbxm.mvs import resolve, Root
from dbxm.model import GA
import collections

root_el = ET.parse("/tmp/claude-1000/-home-curtis-code-imagej-imagej-updater/e8b2271f-24f1-43d1-b743-afdafe4e2ae1/scratchpad/index-fiji3.xml").getroot()
catalog = parse_catalog(root_el)
fiji = GA("sc.fiji", "fiji")
release = catalog.components[fiji].releases[catalog.components[fiji].current]
selection = release.selection
print(f"published selection: {len(selection)} components")

res = resolve(catalog, [Root(fiji, release.version)])
counts = collections.Counter(w.type for w in res.warnings)
print(f"mediated: {len(res.selected)} components, warnings {dict(counts) or 'NONE'}")

extra = {str(k): v for k, v in res.selected.items() if selection.get(k) != v}
missing = {str(k): v for k, v in selection.items() if res.selected.get(k) != v}
print(f"mediated-but-not-selected: {len(extra)}", dict(list(extra.items())[:5]) if extra else "")
print(f"selected-but-not-mediated: {len(missing)}", dict(list(missing.items())[:8]) if missing else "")
print("IDENTITY:", "EXACT" if not extra and not missing else "MISMATCH")
EOF

Claude 2026-07-04 23:13:39

The selection-based design is fully implemented, validated against real Fiji data, and committed in both repos. The headline result:

Single-site identity holds exactly. Mediating the regenerated fiji index with sc.fiji:fiji:2.17.0 as the sole root reproduces the jgo-resolved constellation perfectly — 570 components, zero deviations, zero warnings (down from 67 warnings under the previous edge-driven semantics). A site serving Fiji 2.17.0 now serves exactly what jgo sc.fiji:fiji:2.17.0 resolves, by construction and by verification.

Getting there surfaced one genuine design refinement that your instinct about selections implied but my first implementation missed. The publish-time self-check failed on the first fiji run: the reachability walk over-reached into subgraphs jgo’s resolution had pruned — xalan, eventbus:1.4 (via ancient versions of components it wandered into), Windows-i586 natives — because root-depMgmt exclusions, scope overrides, and platform filtering are pruning decisions the facts edges can’t express. The fix elevates a principle now recorded in the doc: a selection is a membership statement, not just a version map. On-selection nodes follow edges only into the selection’s domain; selection-bearing roots contribute only selection versions; off-selection subtrees (winner-raised or pinned-to-non-offered) traverse freely with edge versions as last resort. That rule is captured in a new regression vector (selection-prunes-overreach), bringing the conformance suite to 18 vectors, all passing in both implementations in both root orders.

State of the world: db-xml-maven main (f8467ef…4d5c134) has the flattener emitting selections via jgo’s actual resolver plus the identity self-check; imagej-updater maven branch (cadc437, f7f12de) has the mirrored Java resolver and updated design doc (decision 9, superseding the fidelity-gap clause of decision 7). Deferred and flagged: per-platform selections (fiji’s selection is currently host-platform-flavored) — bundled with the general client platform-filtering work.

Worth pausing on how the layers of verification each caught something this session: the golden vectors caught nothing (both implementations agreed) — it was the real-data checks that bit: the fiji mediation run exposed the BOM-closure gap, the held-below report exposed the jgo comparator divergence, and the identity self-check exposed the over-reach. The publish-time self-check is clearly load-bearing; it should stay mandatory in the generator, not become an optional flag.

Next in the pipeline, unchanged: merge-not-overwrite emission into an existing db.xml.gz (the enabler for the test-site fork of the Fiji site), then wiring MVSResolver into the client’s update-check flow.
